# PHASE_STATE.md

Single source of truth for project status.
Update at every phase transition. Never leave stale.

See `SESSION_HANDOFF.md` for resume-work context from the most recent
session: gotchas hit, open questions mid-decision, uncommitted changes.
This file stays the authority on phase/gate status if the two disagree.

`ConnectionPool` whose `asyncio.Lock` is taken on every `get_connection()`, while `test_coordinator_integration.py` and `test_operator_api.py` each run the app under their own `TestClient` and therefore their own event loop. **Why it is intermittent is the part worth keeping: `asyncio.Lock.acquire()` reaches `_get_loop()` only on the CONTENDED path**, so an uncontended acquire never binds a loop at all — binding needs two coroutines to want a Redis connection at the same instant, which the coordinator's background heartbeat sweep supplies at unpredictable moments, and once bound it is permanent because neither `ConnectionPool.reset()` nor `Redis.aclose()` replaces the lock. **Fixed in the tests and NOT in the coordinator, deliberately: a deployed coordinator has one event loop for the life of the process, so the production path cannot reach this** — running one process across many loops is a property of the suite alone, and it is the same remedy `test_operator_api.py` already applied to `assignment._work_available`. Two of the three remove work rather than adding it, and none adds a query, a store, a background job, a migration or a protocol message; the Step 3.3 and 3.4 wording each contradicted was corrected in the same commit.** The gate's spine: **lease expiry in Postgres is the single trigger for task recovery** — worker status, socket closes and heartbeats never reclaim, because `_sweep_heartbeats` declares a worker OFFLINE when its Redis key is missing, so an OFFLINE-driven reclaim would reassign the whole in-flight fleet on a Redis blip. A socket close instead *shortens* the lease, accelerating the one mechanism rather than adding a second. Renewal is worker-driven and therefore capped by a coordinator-set per-type `deadline_at` (§12). Fencing is `worker == assigned_worker_id AND attempt_number == attempt_count`; **`session_epoch` is deliberately NOT a fencing input, because Step 2.5 measured a legitimate result executed under epoch 4 and submitted on 5** — one of three §16 escalations raised (the others: no Redis dedup store, and Postgres having no replica/PITR/backup, which M3 does not fix and does not claim to). One migration `0006` for the whole milestone; the ~20,636 pre-M3 stranded `ASSIGNED` rows are closed as `FAILED` by backfill rather than resurrected into a 20k-task flood. The race was reproduced live rather than described, on a **three-replica** stack. Still inherited unchanged: **no remote Internet worker (§8 not claimed) and every demo agent-run rather than user-run (§15 items 3–4)**. **⇒ Step 3.4 (stale result fencing) is BUILT and VERIFIED 2026-08-05 and awaits approval (Decisions #188–#192); Steps 3.5–3.9 remain NOT STARTED and are not to begin without an explicit go-ahead (§9).** 3.4 completes the sentence 3.3 left unfinished: a result is accepted **only** if the sender is the current `assigned_worker_id` **and** its `attempt_number` is the task's current one, so a stale result from an earlier attempt of a task the same worker holds again — accepted until now — is refused, and a late result for a live task reassigned elsewhere is answered `fenced` with a `task_attempts` row rather than `not_owner` with nothing. | 1.5.7 | Coordinator horizontal scaling proof | **DONE — all 6 exit criteria objectively met live on AKS staging, APPROVED by user 2026-07-27.** Design gate = Decision #75 (option A2: raise staging namespace quota via Terraform to keep the 300m coordinator CPU limit, + option B1: synthetic in-cluster HTTP load for the autoscale criterion — no M2 task pipeline exists yet, honest ceiling §10). Changes: `namespace_quota` limits.cpu 2→3 / limits.memory 2Gi→3Gi / requests 1→1.5 / 1Gi→1.5Gi (Terraform apply, in-place, both namespaces — prod scaled down so unused); staging coordinator HPA min 2→3 / max 2→5 (`values-staging.yaml`, deployed via `helm upgrade` on SHA `b1963f90`); new versioned load manifest `infra/loadtest-coordinator.yaml`. **Zero application-code changes** — the phase proves invariant §3.9, already built (Redis connection registry #19, epoch pub/sub eviction #29, DB/Redis-backed read path #30). **Verified live:** (1) ✅ 3 replicas serve one fleet simultaneously (spread across both nodes); (2) ✅ worker distribution verified not assumed — with 6 workers reconnected, each of the 3 replicas served distinct `worker_id`s (per-pod `kubectl logs` `worker_connected`); (3) ✅ killed one replica (`fqjnv`, 4 workers) → orphaned workers reconnected to survivors via Phase-1.7 backoff, replacement pod rescheduled (Deployment min=3), fleet `connected` recovered to 6 in ~30s on the DB-backed `/workers` read path (= dashboard's one read path, #30); (4) ✅ **autoscale both directions** — synthetic load Job (`/ready` flood, in-cluster so the edge `limit-rps` doesn't throttle) drove HPA to **191%/70% → scaled 3→5**; on job completion CPU fell to 3%, HPA held 5 through its ~5-min scaleDown stabilization window, then **scaled 5→3** at 15:16:51; (5) ✅ one coherent fleet regardless of replica count — `/workers` queried from **every** replica returned identical `total=37 connected=6 online=6` (37 = cumulative Postgres registrations, 6 = live workers); (6) ✅ no coordinator holds authoritative state — killed all three original replicas in turn (`fqjnv`,`8klwk`,`bclq7`); after every kill the fleet stayed `total=37 connected=6 online=6` (all state in Postgres/Redis, §3.9). **Honest ceilings:** load is synthetic HTTP, not real task load (M2); autoscale ceiling 5 is bounded by the raised quota + 2-node pool, not tested beyond. Config changes uncommitted (no commit requested). Node left running (user to `az aks stop`). |
| 1.5.8 | Real Internet worker onboarding | **DONE — CPU/mem gap closed via `psutil`; APPROVED by user 2026-07-27. The remaining machine-type criteria (VPS-in-another-country, mobile hotspot, and the simultaneous-multi-worker "all on one dashboard with region-latency deltas" capture) were WAIVED by explicit user direction (Decision #77) — not verified, recorded honestly as a user scope call, same discretion family as #34–35.** Design sub-gate decided (Decision #76): non-Docker distribution = **bootstrap script** (A1), enrollment = **keep shared `ENROLLMENT_SECRET`** (B1). Built offline (node stopped): (a) worker single-instance lock made **cross-platform** (`fcntl` POSIX / `msvcrt` Windows) so the bare-Python worker runs on machines without Docker — the `import fcntl` was the one hard blocker for native Windows; CPU/mem `/proc` reads already degrade to `None` off-Linux; verified by `tests/test_worker_lock.py` (passes on this Windows host, exercising the new msvcrt branch); (b) `worker/install-worker.sh` + `worker/install-worker.ps1` bootstrap installers (clone → venv → run, config = 3 values); (c) `docs/onboarding-a-worker.md` — Docker/Linux/Windows onboarding + operator issue/revoke/rotate procedure. The 3 portability fixes + installers + docs were **committed and shipped through the pipeline** (PR #11 merged to main, CI green, CD deployed staging+production on SHA `42d8c4c`) so the non-Docker installer works from a clone and the worker image is published at that SHA. **Verified live 2026-07-27:** native Windows/no-Docker worker (#3) and **friend's PC installed from docs only on their own ISP (#4)** both `registered`+`ws_connected` through the public ingress (see Real Internet Workers Verified table). **CPU/mem gap CLOSED (Decision #77):** `worker/worker.py` now reads CPU/memory via `psutil` (cross-platform Linux/Windows/macOS), replacing the Linux-only `/proc/stat`+`/proc/meminfo` readers that returned `None` on native Windows/macOS and left the §6 dashboard columns blank. Verified on this native Windows host — `_read_cpu_percent()` returns real values (e.g. 44.4 / 37.5), `_read_memory_percent()` 65.4; worker test suite green (5 passed / 1 skipped); `psutil>=5.9,<7` added to `worker/requirements.txt` and shipped through the pipeline (**PR #12**) so the bootstrap installer + published image carry it. **Machine types verified: 4 of 5** — laptop Docker + 2nd PC (1.5.5, #1/#2), native Windows no-Docker (#3), friend's PC from docs on own ISP (#4). **NOT done and WAIVED by the user (Decision #77):** VPS-in-another-country + mobile-hotspot connections, and the "all workers on one dashboard with region-latency deltas" capture — the user directed 1.5.8 be closed after the CPU/mem fix and explicitly not to pursue the rest. Recorded as a user scope call, not a verified result (§10). |
| 1.5.9 | M1.5 demo and verification | **DONE — SIGNED OFF by user 2026-07-28.** Closed on user direction rather than a fresh end-to-end demo run; recorded honestly per §10 as a user scope call, same discretion family as Decisions #34–35 and #77. Final SHA `4575097157debe48d71b9f3aff86248c599d5b1e` (PR #13 merge), deployed by CD to **both** staging and production — `/health` returned that exact SHA in each. **Exit criteria, honestly:** (1) full demo — **NOT re-run**; user directed it be closed on the evidence already gathered in 1.5.1–1.5.8 (commit→CI→staging deploy timed at 82s in 1.5.4; rolling upgrade with no permanent worker loss in 1.5.2; replica scale up/down with a coherent fleet in 1.5.7; public dashboard + multi-network workers in 1.5.5/1.5.8). (2) failure demo — **partly re-run and newly proven this session**: killing a coordinator pod (1.5.7), auto-rollback on a bad deploy (1.5.4), worker disconnect/reconnect (1.7/1.10), credential revoke → cannot return (1.10, Docker Compose only, never re-proven on AKS), and — **new, previously never tested at any point** — **database offline**: scaled staging `postgres` to 0, all 3 coordinators went `0/1` NotReady with **0 restarts** (liveness correctly independent of the DB), public `/ready` 503, `DatabaseDown` fired at T+3m41s and reached Alertmanager `receiver=chat` (Discord), then full recovery **38s** after restore with 45 worker rows and `alembic_version=0001` intact. Note the `max(...)==0` expr fires only because Prometheus still scrapes NotReady pods — it works today but lacks the `absent()` guard `CoordinatorDown` got in 1.5.6 C3. Also learned: while all coordinators are unready the ingress has no upstream, so the **entire public surface including the dashboard returns 503**. (3) five machine types — **4 of 5**; VPS-in-another-country and mobile hotspot **WAIVED** (Decision #77). (4) staging reproducible from Terraform — **improved but never proven from nothing**: the secrets gap is closed (nine SealedSecrets committed, validated 9/9 from a fresh clone), `terraform plan` is now a trustworthy signal (4 phantom changes → 1 real), and the clone→AKS sequence is documented; a true from-destroyed rebuild was never attempted (would cost credit and destroy the environment). (5) runbook — **DONE**, `docs/runbook.md`. (6) cost tracked against estimate — **NOT DONE**, no cost figures were ever recorded. (7) `PHASE_STATE.md` updated — this entry. (8) approval before Phase 2 — given 2026-07-28. |

> **Milestone 1.5 direction (current, 2026-07-24): Microsoft Azure —
> AKS (Azure Kubernetes Service), Free-tier control plane** (Decisions
> Log #57, supersedes local k3d + Cloudflare Tunnel #52–#53). The system
> deploys to a **managed AKS cluster** on an **Azure for Students**
> subscription ($100 credit / 12 months / no card). Because student
> credit is finite, cost discipline is mandatory and explicit: AKS
> **Free-tier control plane ($0)**, a **single small B-series burstable
> node with no autoscaling**, **`az aks stop` whenever a test session
> ends** (deallocates node VMs → compute billing halts), and
> `az group delete` to remove everything. A real managed cloud has a real
> public LoadBalancer/IP, so **Cloudflare Tunnel is retired** (same
> reasoning as the earlier Decision #49). M1.5's Kubernetes/Helm/CI-CD
> content is fully real again — "local cluster" becomes "managed AKS
> cluster", and reachability is a real Azure public endpoint (spun up
> only while demoing to conserve credit). The k3d-era `infra/terraform/`
> (thin, `kubernetes`/`helm` only) is rewritten at Step 1.5.1 to add the
> **`azurerm` provider** (resource group + AKS + node pool); its short
> design sub-gate settles two open sub-choices (Open Questions #3):
> whether Terraform provisions the cluster itself vs `az aks create` +
> thin Terraform, and the node VM size. Provider-agnostic Decisions
> #39–41 / #45–46 (self-hosted Postgres/Redis, staging/production
> namespaces, sealed-secrets, ghcr.io, Terraform Cloud remote state)
> still stand — no ACR, no Key Vault, no Azure Storage backend. **All
> prior M1.5 build work (done on k3d) is scrapped (Decision #58);
> Steps 1.5.1–1.5.9 are NOT STARTED and rebuilt from scratch on Azure.**

### Milestone 2 — Task Distribution
| Phase | Title | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 2.0 | Design gate — queue and assignment model | **DONE — approved by user 2026-07-28 (Decisions #79–#82).** (A) Queue = **PostgreSQL `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`** on the task table, not Redis — Redis runs with no persistence by Decision #39 and nothing rebuilds a lost queue, and even with AOF added the Redis/Postgres dual-write breaks §3.7. (B) Assignment = **hybrid**: worker advertises `max_concurrent` credits, coordinator pushes over the existing 1.5 pub/sub path — keeps assignment coordinator-authoritative for M4 (§3.3) while borrowing pull's backpressure. (C) Results = separate `task_results` table, recommended 64 KB cap / 7-day body retention. (D) Type extensibility = coordinator-side type registry; **envelope unchanged, `PROTOCOL_VERSION` stays `1.0`**, new `message_type` values only (Decision #6's stated mechanism). All three exit criteria met. **Consequence:** Step 2.2 renamed "Redis-backed queue" → "Durable task queue" in both this register and `docs/phase-2-task-distribution.md`; the old title pre-judged a choice Step 2.0 explicitly left open, and was flagged to the user as a §16 escalation before approval. **Unmeasured (§10):** every throughput/latency claim behind (A) is reasoning, not measurement, and must be verified by 2.2's 10,000-task criterion and the 2.8 harness. |
| 2.1 | Task model, schema, state machine | **DONE — APPROVED by user 2026-07-28.** Approval covered the two judgement calls raised at review: (a) `task_results` created in the 2.1 migration rather than 2.5's, so 2.5 needs no schema change; (b) `priority` is lower-is-more-urgent (Unix nice), so one ascending index serves the whole `ORDER BY`. **Committed and CI-verified 2026-07-28, NOT YET MERGED OR DEPLOYED.** Branch `phase-2.1-task-model` (2 commits: `32dfb69` feature, `a676c24` docs), **PR #15**, opened **stacked on PR #14** (base `docs/m15-signoff`, so the diff shows only M2 work; GitHub retargets to `main` once #14 merges). **CI green — all 7 required checks pass** (run `30346617672`): lint, test, build ×3, scan, terraform. **The CI test job is the second, independent proof of the migration:** it ran **81 passed with no skips**, meaning the integration test executed and drove `alembic upgrade head` — including `0002` — through the app's real FastAPI lifespan against CI's ephemeral Postgres, not just the laptop verification below. (The single pytest warning is **pre-existing**: the last CI run on `main`, SHA `4575097`, reported "9 passed, 1 warning" before any of this work.) **MERGED AND FULLY DEPLOYED 2026-07-28.** User started the AKS cluster (2 nodes, `Running`); PR #14 (M1.5 sign-off docs) and PR #15 (this work) were both merged. `main` is now **`2c50bae`**, CI green on it (run `30347586094`), SHA-tagged images pushed to ghcr.io. Both CD runs completed **success on both environments** — `30347488231` (`d41058e`) and `30347651705` (`2c50bae`), each with `staging / deploy` and `production / deploy` green after the user approved the `production` required-reviewer gates. **Verified live, not inferred:** staging `/health` returns `2c50baea029c80a102af123a3f23b02ad80a1ba0`; the **staging database is at `alembic_version = 0002` with tables `workers`, `tasks`, `task_results`**; **production is likewise at `0002`** with coordinator image `…:2c50bae…`. **This is the third independent environment in which migration `0002` has now applied** — the laptop `upgrade/downgrade/upgrade` run, CI's ephemeral Postgres via the app lifespan, and now real AKS staging + production via the Kubernetes initContainer (Decision #55). **Note on sequencing observed during the merge:** `cd.yml` sets `concurrency: group cd-main, cancel-in-progress: false`, so the `2c50bae` CD run sat `pending` with zero jobs until the *earlier* run's production gate was resolved. Deploys are strictly serialised behind that gate — expected behaviour, worth knowing before assuming a queued deploy has failed. All 5 exit criteria objectively verified; nothing asserted. New: `coordinator/app/task_states.py` (state machine), `coordinator/app/task_types.py` (four dummy types + parameter registry), `Task`/`TaskResult` in `coordinator/app/models.py`, migration `0002_create_tasks_tables.py`, `tests/test_task_states.py` + `tests/test_task_types.py`, root `conftest.py`. **(1) Migration applies cleanly and is reversible — MEASURED**, not asserted: against an ephemeral `postgres:16-alpine`, `alembic upgrade head` ran `-> 0001 -> 0002` clean on an empty DB (`alembic current` = `0002 (head)`, tables `tasks`+`task_results` present with all 15/4 spec columns), then `downgrade 0001` dropped both tables leaving `workers` intact and **0 orphan indexes**, then `upgrade head` re-applied clean. **(2) Invalid transitions rejected in code — 30 tests**, incl. skip-ahead (`QUEUED→RUNNING`, `ASSIGNED→COMPLETED`), backwards moves, terminal states never moving on, and worker-lifecycle states (`ONLINE`/`OFFLINE`/…) rejected as unknown so the two state machines cannot share a vocabulary through the shared free-text column. Same-state is a **no-op returning False, not an error** — that is what makes duplicate result submission harmless (§3.7). **(3) Four task types validate and reject malformed input — 42 tests**: missing/negative/over-ceiling/wrong-type values, unoffered `algorithm`, non-base64 payload, over-cap payload, unknown type, and **unknown keys rejected not ignored** (`extra="forbid"`, §12). **(4) `lease_expires_at` + `attempt_count` exist, written by nothing in M2** — confirmed present in the live schema dump. **(5) `correlation_id` NOT NULL** — confirmed `null=NO` in the live schema. **Beyond the criteria, Decision #79's index design was smoke-checked and holds:** with 20,000 rows (18,000 `QUEUED`), the real dequeue (`WHERE status='QUEUED' ORDER BY priority, created_at LIMIT 10 FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`) plans as `Index Scan using ix_tasks_queue` with **no sort node** — the index supplies the ordering — at 0.15ms execution. **Labelled honestly (§10): that is a smoke check on a laptop, not Step 2.2's 10,000-task criterion and not the 2.8 load harness.** Full suite **77 passed / 1 skipped** (integration skipped, needs `POSTGRES_HOST`); `ruff check` clean on the CI paths. **Two judgement calls to confirm at approval:** (a) `task_results` is created in *this* migration rather than 2.5's, so 2.5 needs no schema change — same reasoning as the reserved Phase 3 columns; (b) `priority` is **lower-is-more-urgent** (Unix nice), so one ascending index serves the whole `ORDER BY`. Also: root `conftest.py` added because coordinator modules import as `app.*` via CI's `PYTHONPATH` and a bare local `pytest` could not resolve them — local and CI runs now agree. `pydantic>=2,<3` pinned explicitly in `coordinator/requirements.txt` (was transitive via fastapi; validation now depends on v2 semantics directly). **Not verified: nothing has run in CI or on AKS — no commit, no PR, no deploy this session.** |
| 2.2 | Durable task queue (Postgres, Decision #79 — was titled "Redis-backed queue" before the 2.0 gate) | **DONE — APPROVED by user 2026-07-28.** Approval covered Decisions #83–#85, including the judgement call on the `POST /tasks/dequeue` endpoint: kept as a queue *primitive* (the caller names the worker; it makes no scheduling decision), since Step 2.3's engine calls the same `task_queue.dequeue()` function and without the endpoint the three-replica criterion could not be proven against real replicas at all. **The approval review also surfaced a real security defect that became Step 2.2.1** — `verify_admin_secret` compared against the shared `ENROLLMENT_SECRET`, so every worker could call every admin endpoint including the new queue ones. New: `coordinator/app/task_queue.py` (enqueue / enqueue_batch / dequeue / queue_depth / counts_by_status), three admin-guarded endpoints (`POST /tasks`, `GET /tasks/depth`, `POST /tasks/dequeue`), a `coordinator_tasks_queued` Prometheus gauge, `/tasks` added to the coordinator ingress paths, `tests/test_task_queue.py` (11 tests), and the versioned harness `scripts/queue_harness.py`. **No migration** — Phase 2.1's schema and `ix_tasks_queue` were sufficient, which was the point of reserving them. **Verification ran against three real coordinator processes over TLS sharing one Postgres, driven over HTTP by the harness** (not three threads in one process, and not direct SQL). **(1) 10,000 tasks enqueue and dequeue without loss — COUNTED:** enqueued 10,000 in **0.798s**; drained **10,025 claimed / 10,025 unique / 0 duplicates** (the extra 25 were survivors from the preceding restart test, which is itself evidence they persisted); depth returned to 0; throughput **1,106 tasks/sec**; zero request errors. **(2) Concurrent dequeuers never double-assign — VERIFIED UNDER LOAD:** 0 duplicates across two independent 10,000-task drains, with work genuinely spread across all three coordinators (3370/3305/3350, then 3330/3370/3300). Also proven at test scale in CI by `test_concurrent_dequeuers_never_double_assign_and_never_lose_a_task` (600 tasks, 8 concurrent sessions, each on its own connection). **Then PROVEN ON THREE REAL AKS PODS 2026-07-28** — merged as PR #16, CI green on all 7 required checks (**92 passed, no skips**, so the queue tests ran against CI's ephemeral Postgres — the second independent proof), `main` at **`2d9b686`**, CD auto-deployed staging, and the versioned Job `infra/loadtest-queue.yaml` ran the harness in-cluster against the `coordinator` Service (3 pods, HPA min 3 from Step 1.5.7). Result: **10,000 claimed / 10,000 unique / 0 duplicates**, 975.8 tasks/sec, enqueue 0.896s, depth read 15ms, zero errors — and `by_coordinator_instance` **named all three pods** (`s87nh` 3430, `dtv5r` 3400, `684cv` 3170), so three replicas taking part is evidence, not an inference from three pods being Running. **(3) Depth accurate and cheap — MEASURED, and the first claim written was wrong:** the docstring originally asserted an "index range count, not a table scan"; `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` showed a **Seq Scan** (3.6ms, 560 buffers) immediately after a bulk load, because autovacuum had not yet updated the visibility map. Corrected in the code and recorded here (§10). Steady state at **320,025 rows with 10,000 queued: 2.4ms**, and the cost tracks *queued* rows rather than table size (Bitmap Heap Scan touching 214 heap blocks), which is the property that matters for a table that accumulates terminal rows forever. A partial index was built and measured as an alternative — 17x smaller (88 kB vs 1552 kB) but **no faster** in steady state, so it was **not** taken (Decision #85). **(4) Restarting all replicas loses no queued task — VERIFIED:** with 10,000 QUEUED / 10,025 ASSIGNED / 300,000 COMPLETED, all three coordinator processes were killed (`Stop-Process -Force`, no graceful shutdown), restarted, and depth read back **identical on every count**; the queue then drained completely, 10,000 claimed / 0 duplicates, proving the surviving rows were still claimable and not merely present. **(5) Ordering guarantee stated and verified by test:** stated in `task_queue`'s docstring and in `docs/phase-2-task-distribution.md` — a single dequeuer gets strict `(priority ASC, created_at ASC)`; under N concurrent dequeuers order is deliberately *not* globally total because `SKIP LOCKED` is what buys the concurrency, and what holds instead is no duplication, no loss, no starvation. Tested by `test_a_single_dequeuer_gets_strict_priority_then_fifo_order`, which commits each enqueue separately on purpose: Postgres `now()` is the **transaction** timestamp, so tasks enqueued in one transaction share a `created_at` and have no defined order among themselves — the honest limit of the FIFO half, documented rather than hidden. The survivors were then drained: **5,001 claimed / 0 duplicates** across the three *new* pods, proving the rows were still claimable and not merely present. **Ordering re-proven live:** priorities enqueued 5,1,5,0,1 and drained one at a time through the public endpoint came out **0,1,1,5,5**. **Failure demos, all live against the public endpoint:** unknown task type → 400, negative parameter → 400, unknown parameter key → 400, batch over the 10,000 cap → 400, base64 payload over the 64 KB cap → 400, wrong admin secret → 401, absent admin secret → 401, valid task → 201. **§3.9 confirmed for the new metric:** `coordinator_tasks_queued` read from all three replicas returned **42, 42, 42**, matching `GET /tasks/depth` exactly — recomputed from Postgres per scrape, nothing cached in process memory. **§11 confirmed end to end:** structured JSON logs show `tasks_enqueued` and `task_dequeued` **sharing one `correlation_id`** (`ef984c4f…`), with `task_id`, `worker_id` and `coordinator_instance` on the dequeue. **Whole-system regression check, not just tests:** a **real worker on this laptop connected over the public Internet** (`registered` → `access_token_refreshed` → `ws_connected`, `session_epoch` 1, held ~10 min with no reconnect), showed ONLINE on `/workers` with live CPU 34.1% / memory 49.2% / latency 78.7 ms alongside the in-cluster demo worker; the dashboard returned 401 anonymous and 200 with basic auth, and `/api/workers` served the fleet. A real task was then **enqueued through the public ingress and claimed for that Internet-connected worker** — the Postgres row reads `status=ASSIGNED`, `assigned_worker_id=7c5e5024…`, `assigned_at` stamped, `parameters` normalised with the registry default filled in, and — the Phase 2.1 criterion holding under real load — **`lease_expires_at` NULL and `attempt_count` 0**. **PRODUCTION DEPLOYED AND INDEPENDENTLY VERIFIED 2026-07-28.** The user approved both parked CD gates; runs `30353450893` (`2d9b686`) and `30355381076` (`5e66a9b`) both completed **success on staging and production**, so **both environments now run `5e66a9b`**. A 26-check suite was then executed **inside a real coordinator pod in each namespace** — against the deployed image over the pod's own TLS listener, not a local build — and returned **26/26 pass in production and 26/26 in staging**: liveness/readiness, 401 on wrong and on missing admin credential, 400 for unknown task type / negative parameter / unknown parameter key / over-cap batch / over-cap base64 payload, a full enqueue→depth→register→dequeue→drained round trip, `coordinator_tasks_queued` exported and agreeing with the HTTP depth, and the fleet gauges still exported. **Concurrency re-proven separately in each environment against its own real replicas** — production **400 claimed / 0 duplicates split 165/235 across both pods**, staging **400 claimed / 0 duplicates split 170/140/90 across all three**. **Schema confirmed identical in both:** `alembic_version = 0002`, tables `alembic_version`/`task_results`/`tasks`/`workers`, indexes `ix_tasks_queue`/`ix_tasks_assigned_worker_id`/`ix_tasks_correlation_id` — Step 2.2 needed no migration and added none. **The Phase 2.1 reservation holds across the entire table, not just a sample:** over all 15,501 staging task rows, **0** have `lease_expires_at` set, **0** have `attempt_count ≠ 0`, and **0** have a NULL `correlation_id`. **Public surface re-verified with no `-k` anywhere** (so the certificate genuinely validated): `/health` returns `5e66a9b…`, `/ready` db+redis ok, the new `/tasks` route serves through the ingress, dashboard 401 anonymous / 200 with basic auth, `/api/workers` 200; the certificate is a real Let's Encrypt one (`CN=YR1, O=Let's Encrypt`) for `dcds-staging.centralindia.cloudapp.azure.com`, valid to 2026-10-22. **Edge rate limiting still enforced:** 15 rapid registration attempts → exactly **5 reached the app (401) and 10 were rejected at nginx (429)**, matching `limit-rps: 5`. **Observability intact:** all 10 pods Running, and **Prometheus is scraping `coordinator_tasks_queued` from all three staging pods** (confirmed by querying the Prometheus API directly, not by assuming the ServiceMonitor works). **M1 regression, end to end:** a real worker on this laptop over the public Internet reaffirmed its persisted identity → `access_token_refreshed` → `ws_connected` (epoch 1), showed ONLINE with CPU 42.8 / memory 52.6 / latency 72.4 ms, a task enqueued through the public ingress was claimed for it (row: `ASSIGNED`, correct worker, `assigned_at` stamped, `lease_expires_at` NULL, `attempt_count` 0), and **one `correlation_id` (`c0a60dc4…`) spans `tasks_enqueued` on pod `pdpwz` and `task_dequeued` on pod `grk7b` — two different replicas** (§11). | 2.2.1 | Operator credential separation (security fix, inserted between 2.2 and 2.3) | **DONE — APPROVED by user 2026-07-28.** Approved on the evidence gathered rather than on a personally-run demo: the user was offered the three-command check and explicitly chose to approve on condition the step works as intended, which it was measured to do. Recorded honestly as a **user scope call** on §15 items 3 and 4 (demo and failure demo performed by the user personally), the same discretion family as Decisions #34–35 and #77 — not as a verified result. Everything else in §15 is objectively met. **The approval rests on a specific fact, checked rather than assumed: the code has not changed since the verification.** `git diff bdb556d..61ecc4c` touches only `PHASE_STATE.md` and `SESSION_HANDOFF.md`, so the 37/37 runs in both environments were executed against exactly the code that is deployed and running.** Opened because the Step 2.2 approval review found a real defect (Decision #86): `verify_admin_secret` compared against `ENROLLMENT_SECRET`, the **shared** bootstrap credential every worker holds by design (Decision #76 B1), so **every worker could call every admin endpoint** — list the whole fleet, revoke or push to any peer, and after Step 2.2 drain the entire task queue and self-assign all of it, against §12. **Pre-existing since Phase 1.4** (`/workers/{id}/revoke` had it, and `config`'s own docstring flagged it as deferred); Step 2.2 is what made it worth fixing now, since the blast radius went from griefing peers to taking all the work and stops being bounded once Step 2.4 executes real work. **Fix:** a distinct `ADMIN_SECRET`, never given to a worker; the dashboard carries it (it is an operator tool), the worker does not, and neither does the migrate initContainer, which serves no request. It **falls back to `ENROLLMENT_SECRET` when unset** so an image can roll out before the Secret is applied — but the fallback logs `admin_secret_fallback_in_use` at WARNING and exports `coordinator_admin_credential_separate 0`, so insecure-but-silent is not an available state. Neither secret is ever logged, only whether they differ. New sealed secrets committed for both namespaces (§13, Decision #78). CI runs with the two set to **different** values so it exercises the separated posture; the fallback has its own unit tests. Merged as **PR #19**, CI green on all 7 required checks (**101 passed**, up from 92 — 9 new tests), `main` at **`fabb012`**, CD deployed staging. **All 6 exit criteria objectively verified, live on AKS staging, not asserted.** (1) **A worker's enrollment secret is rejected by every admin endpoint** — 401 on `GET /workers`, `GET /tasks/depth`, `POST /tasks`, `POST /tasks/dequeue`, `POST /workers/{id}/revoke` and `POST /workers/{id}/push`, checked both through the public ingress and from inside a pod. (2) **The operator credential is accepted** — 200/201 on the same endpoints. (3) **The fleet is not locked out** — a real worker on this laptop over the public Internet, with **no `ADMIN_SECRET` anywhere in its environment**, reaffirmed its identity → `access_token_refreshed` → `ws_connected` (epoch 1) and showed ONLINE with live CPU 30.3 / memory 68.2 / latency 73.4 ms; registration with the enrollment secret returns 201 while the **operator secret is rejected for enrollment (401)**, so the split cuts both ways. (4) **The dashboard still reads the fleet** — 401 anonymous, 200 with basic auth, and `/api/workers` returned 59 workers, confirming the operator credential reached it. (5) **A deployment without `ADMIN_SECRET` still serves and says so** — proven against a real coordinator over TLS: `/health` 200, admin endpoints answer on the enrollment secret, `admin_secret_fallback_in_use` logged at WARNING, gauge `0`. (6) **The posture is observable without reading a log** — `coordinator_admin_credential_separate` returned **1.0 on all three staging replicas**, and each logged `admin_credential_separate` at startup. **Full regression on staging: 37/37 checks pass** (the 26 from Step 2.2 plus 11 separation checks), including the concurrency proof re-run after the change — 400 tasks claimed, **0 duplicates**, split 130/135/135 across all three replicas. The versioned harness Job was re-run with the new two-credential wiring: **3,000 tasks, 0 duplicates**, 934 tasks/sec, all three pods serving. A real task was enqueued and claimed for the Internet worker with `lease_expires_at` NULL and `attempt_count` 0 still holding. **Confirmed no secret leaks:** neither credential appears in any log line, and the committed sealed secrets contain no plaintext. **PRODUCTION FIXED AND MEASURED 2026-07-28.** The user approved the parked CD gates; both environments now run **`6eb32ca`**. The decisive measurement, taken inside a production pod rather than read off CD's green tick: the enrollment secret on production's `/tasks/depth` went from **200 before the fix to 401 after it**, and returns 401 on every other admin endpoint too, while the operator credential returns 200/201 and workers still enroll (201). `ADMIN_SECRET` was confirmed present in the production container and different from `ENROLLMENT_SECRET`. **Full regression re-run in both environments after the change: production 37/37, staging 37/37**, each including its own concurrency proof against its own replicas — production 400 tasks / **0 duplicates** split 190/210 across both pods, staging 400 / 0 across all three. **`coordinator_admin_credential_separate` reads 1.0 on all five replicas across both environments, and every one logged `admin_credential_separate` at startup — not a single pod is running on the fallback.** Public surface re-checked with no `-k`: `/health` on `6eb32ca`, the worker secret 401 on `/tasks/depth` through the ingress, dashboard 401 anonymous / 200 authenticated serving a 65-worker fleet, and the edge rate limit still admitting exactly 5 before 429. **The defect is closed in every environment.** **Two follow-ups shipped the same day (PR #22, `main` at `bdb556d`, deployed to both environments, regression re-run 37/37 in each):** (a) **the `ADMIN_SECRET` rotation procedure is now written down** in `docs/runbook.md` — the capability existed but the steps did not, which is the difference between being able to rotate and being able to rotate under pressure; it includes the collision check against `ENROLLMENT_SECRET`, the no-plaintext-in-the-commit check, and the post-rotation verification that the old value is rejected, workers are unaffected, and no replica fell back. **Documented but not yet exercised end to end as a real rotation (§10)** — though its trickiest step, offline sealing against the committed public cert, was proven when the secret was created (`Synced=True` in both namespaces). (b) **`client_ip` is now logged on every admin call, accepted and rejected alike**, from `X-Forwarded-For` with a socket-peer fallback — **verified through the real ingress**, where a rejected and an accepted admin call from the public Internet both logged the caller's genuine public address rather than a cluster-internal one. It is a hint, never authentication. Suite **108 passed** (was 101). **A finding recorded rather than silently fixed:** registration's pre-existing `source_ip` is the raw socket peer because it feeds the rate limiter, so behind the ingress **every external worker shares one registration rate-limit bucket**; changing it would fix the bucketing and simultaneously make the limit evadable, so it is left as an explicit decision for the user (documented in `middleware.py`). The edge `limit-rps` at nginx remains the unspoofable primary control. |

Delivery was immediate (enqueue and assignment share the same 06:16:53 second), so the doorbell fired rather than the 30s poll. **(2) No task assigned to two workers — 500 tasks across 50 workers: 500 delivered, 500 unique, 0 duplicates, 500 acknowledged, all 50 workers served, exactly 10 each (the credit ceiling, hit precisely), 0.76s.** Confirmed **independently of the harness's own bookkeeping**: Postgres reported 500 rows / 500 distinct task ids / 50 distinct workers / min 10 / max 10 per worker, and the coordinator's structured logs held **504 `task_assigned` events with 504 distinct task ids and 0 id repeated** (504 = the 500 plus the four single-task probes run before it). `task_acknowledged` came to **503** — exactly 504 minus the one deliberately stranded task, which is the cross-check that the ack count is real and not assumed. **(3) 100 idle workers produce negligible load — MEASURED, and attributed rather than hand-waved:** over the same 60-second window with an empty queue, **1 idle worker and 100 idle workers both produced 2 assignment passes and 0 dequeue queries**. RSS ~118 MB in both. **(4) A queued task with no eligible worker stays queued and visible — and the filter was shown to be selective, not merely inert:** with the worker restarted declaring only `count_to_n`, 3 `sleep` tasks sat at `QUEUED` and stayed visible in `GET /tasks/depth`, while 2 `count_to_n` tasks enqueued at the same moment went straight to `ASSIGNED` on that same worker. Final state `ASSIGNED count_to_n 3 / QUEUED sleep 3` — nothing dropped, nothing dead-lettered. **(5) Disconnect between assignment and acknowledgement is logged — produced deterministically**, via the harness's `stranded` mode rather than by trying to win a millisecond-wide race: `task_unacknowledged_at_disconnect` at WARNING carrying the task id, worker id, session epoch and **the task's correlation id**.
