Implementation decisions **#105–#108**. Suite **203 passed** (was 136 at 2.3), `ruff` clean. **Measured, not asserted.** **(1) All four types execute and return correct results:** live fingerprints matched values computed independently — `count_to_n` `36877f3dcf65`, `hash_rounds` `adcac83e02f9`, `opaque_payload` `42898f37607b`, and `sleep`'s resolved to exactly `14.0` for a 14s task. At scale **1,000 sequential `count_to_n` tasks, 1,000 distinct ids, every one `81a83544cf93`** — the independently computed value for the result `2000`; and **55 of 55 ceiling `hash_rounds` tasks all `2c7324ca2eca`**, matching a 10,000,000-round digest recomputed from scratch outside the system. **(2) The 10-minute criterion, both parts, in ONE window** (harsher than running them apart — one slot held by `sleep(600)` while the other ran ceiling `hash_rounds` back to back on a single CPU): the letter — **one `sleep(600)` task completed, elapsed 600.017s**; the substance — **55 ceiling tasks, 805.5s cumulative CPU-bound execution**, per-task min/mean/max 11.60 / 14.65 / 25.23s. Across the whole 752.4s window the worker sent **150 heartbeats, max gap 5.26s (mean 5.05s), ZERO gaps over the 12s SUSPECT threshold, and ZERO transitions out of `ONLINE`** — the only transitions logged for it are the initial REGISTERED→CONNECTING→ONLINE, 51s before the run began. **Sizing was re-derived on this machine as the criterion requires** (one ceiling task measured **12.916s** here, so 47 covered 600s and 60 were enqueued for margin); the bench machine's `~40` was deliberately not reused. **(3) Progress:** 142 samples in that window; a 14s `sleep` reported 0.25 → 0.61 → 0.97, each carrying the task's own correlation id, with **no Postgres write for any sample**. **(4) Concurrency respected and configurable:** 6 tasks at a worker declaring `max_concurrent: 2` — every start reported `tasks_in_flight` of 1 or 2, never 3, zero refusals. **(5) Local state deleted after submission:** the worker's `running` map and the coordinator's Redis `current_tasks` key both return to empty; the dashboard shows the worker idle. **(6) Memory flat:** RSS **31,184 kB → 31,488 kB (+0.3 MB)** across 1,000 sequential tasks. **(7) Both refusal paths, live**, forced via `POST /workers/{id}/push` because the eligibility filter and credit accounting stop the coordinator over-committing on its own: an unregistered type refused `unsupported_task_type`, and a third task at a full 2-slot worker refused `at_capacity` — refused, never queued locally. **(8) The failure path, live, with an INJECTED fault** — there is no natural way to make an executor raise, which is itself the point (the two type registries agree), so a throwaway worker ran a patched `executors.py` raising `ZeroDivisionError`; **no production code was changed to produce it.** Task reached `FAILED`, credit freed, and the exception *message* appeared nowhere: log and wire carried `error_type` only (§12). **(9) Reconnect under running work:** two 75s tasks kept computing through a **coordinator restart**, the new session's `hello` declared `tasks_in_flight: 2` so the coordinator opened at zero free credits, a task enqueued meanwhile correctly stayed `QUEUED`, and both credits were released **on the new session** the moment the work finished — the queued task then assigned within 100 ms. **That last test found a real bug, and it is the reason it was worth running (Decision #108).** The first implementation captured the session's socket when execution began, so completions after a reconnect reported down a socket the coordinator had already discarded; the sends failed silently, the credits were never released, and the worker sat permanently and invisibly "full" while the queue waited behind it — #101's failure mode by another route. **Neither unit tests nor review caught it; a live reconnect did.** A second §12 hole was found by self-review and closed the same way: a `task_failed` naming another worker's task no longer draws down the reporter's own credits. **What 2.4 deliberately does NOT do**, recorded so it is not mistaken for an oversight: **no task reaches `COMPLETED`** (#105 — a successful task stays `RUNNING` until Step 2.5 persists a result, exactly as 2.3's end state was `ASSIGNED`); **no execution timeout exists** (#103); `lease_expires_at` and `attempt_count` remain **written by nothing**, verified in the database after a full started→progress→failed cycle; and a task surviving a reconnect reports no *progress* to the new session until it finishes, since that session never saw it start — its completion still lands and its credit is still released. **--- The design sub-gate record, unchanged, follows. ---** **DESIGN SUB-GATE ACCEPTED WITH AMENDMENTS 2026-07-30 — Decisions #93–#104. Implementation is authorised but has NOT begun, held by explicit user instruction.** The approval was **delegated to the agent by the user** (a user scope call on §15 item 8), so — stated because it limits what the approval is worth (§10) — **it is self-review, not independent validation.** Run against the shipped code rather than the gate's own prose, it found one real defect and one omission, which is why the verdict is "with amendments" and not "as-is": **#101 supersedes #97, which livelocked** — #97 introduced a *frequent* refusal cause into a path Step 2.3 built for a *rare* one, and because a refusal frees the credit and rings the doorbell (`assignment.py:287-298`) while `assign_once` takes any session with free credits (`assignment.py:398-400`) and leaves the task `ASSIGNED`, a worker with full local slots would be handed task after task, **permanently stranding every one in `ASSIGNED` at loop speed**, with no Phase 3 in M2 to reclaim them; **#102 closes a gap the gate never addressed** — an executor that raises now reports `task_failed` → `FAILED`, since otherwise a crashed task sits `RUNNING` forever in M2; **#103** records that 2.4 adds no execution timeout, duration already being bounded by Step 2.1's validation. **#93–#96, #98 and #100 survived review unchanged.** The measured evidence below is the original gate record (#93–#99). Every design premise was measured rather than assumed, inside the deployed base image (`python:3.12-slim`) constrained to one CPU, via the versioned `scripts/exec_isolation_bench.py`. Decisive results: inline execution makes the heartbeat gap equal the whole task duration (24.58s task, **one** heartbeat where 4.9 were due, SUSPECT breached), while `asyncio.to_thread` held it at 5.01s / 5.19s / 5.47s / 5.65s at 1 / 4 / 4×`count_to_n` / 8 concurrent tasks — never closer than 6.3s to SUSPECT. `ProcessPoolExecutor` was measured and **lost** (68.32s vs 58.04s, 0.85x, no heartbeat gain). Cooperative cancel stops a thread in 0.04–0.11s; progress is readable from a GIL-atomic list slot with no thread-safety machinery; chunking costs nothing measurable; 300 sequential tasks moved RSS 28.4 → 28.4 MB. **The one §16 escalation this gate raised is RESOLVED (Decision #100, option (c), user-chosen 2026-07-30): the "10-minute task" exit criterion is unreachable with a CPU workload at the declared parameter ceilings** — `count_to_n` at 100M runs 8.4s and `hash_rounds` at 10M runs 15.4s, while ten minutes of CPU would need ~388M rounds, ~38x the ceiling. Only `sleep` reaches ten minutes, and a sleeping task proves least about heartbeat survival — so the criterion is now two required parts: a `sleep(600)` task for the letter, **plus** the worker held CPU-saturated for ≥600s by repeated ceiling-parameter `hash_rounds` tasks for the substance, passing on ≥600s continuous execution, zero transitions out of `ONLINE`, and no heartbeat gap over 12s. No ceiling is raised and no Step 2.1 artefact is touched. **Honest ceiling recorded with the decision (§10): threads buy heartbeat survival, not throughput — 4 concurrent CPU tasks on one core measured 1.03x versus serial, i.e. nothing.** |
| 2.5 | Result submission and completion | **BUILT and VERIFIED LOCALLY 2026-07-31 — AWAITING APPROVAL.** All 6 exit criteria met against a real coordinator, worker, Postgres and Redis over TLS in Docker. Design decisions **#110–#117**. Suite **251 passed** (was 203 at 2.4), `ruff` clean. **This is the step that finally completes a task**: 2.4 computed results and discarded them (#98), so every successful task stopped at `RUNNING`; a success now submits a result envelope, the coordinator validates and persists it, and the task reaches `COMPLETED` with `completed_at` stamped and `result_id` set. **Measured, not asserted.** **(1) Results persist and tasks reach COMPLETED:** all four types verified against independently computed answers — `count_to_n(2000)` → `2000`, `hash_rounds(200000)` → `c4773d4f7ba4ea18ce27038cca89df4b29a1d5c12808fd05ea0037fb2579fecd` recomputed from a 200,000-round SHA-256 chain outside the system, `opaque_payload` → its exact base64 round trip, `sleep(4)` → `4.0`. At volume: **200 tasks → 200 COMPLETED, 200 result rows, 200 distinct result ids, 200 distinct idempotency tokens, 0 wrong answers**, with worker RSS **31,224 → 31,412 kB (+0.19 MB)** so the new bounded buffer does not leak. **(2) Idempotency token and session epoch present from day one, enforced by nothing:** every stored envelope carries `attempt_number`, `session_epoch` and `idempotency_token`, and duplicate suppression comes from the task's own terminal state under a `FOR UPDATE` lock instead — three identical submissions over the real socket produced acks `transitioned`, `duplicate`, `duplicate` and **one** result row. **(3) The outage criterion, live:** a `sleep(25)` task was started, the **coordinator was stopped** 6s in, the task finished with nowhere to send (`pending: 1`, backoff climbing 2.35 → 15.16 → 26.23s), and on restart the result was delivered and the task reached `COMPLETED`. Its stored envelope reads **`duration_seconds` 25.002 against an observed 68.67s** — the two differing by the outage is the clearest illustration of why both are recorded — and its `session_epoch` is **4**, the epoch it *executed* under, not the 5 it submitted on. **(4) Malformed rejected without corrupting task state, over the real socket**, driven by a throwaway protocol client so that **no production code was changed to produce it** (the same discipline as 2.4's injected fault): a result with no idempotency token was answered `accepted: false / rejected / missing_idempotency_token`, and the row afterwards was **`RUNNING`, `result_id` NULL, `completed_at` NULL, zero result rows** — untouched. The log carried the reason and never the body (§12). **(5) Execution duration recorded and visible** via the new `GET /tasks/{task_id}`, which reports the worker-reported `duration_seconds` and the coordinator-observed `observed_duration_seconds` **without blurring them** (§10, §12) — the second includes delivery and the result round trip and is the one that cannot be lied about. **(6) Large payloads:** a full-size `opaque_payload` (65,536 decoded bytes) produced an **87,384-character result stored whole** in an 87,602-byte envelope, `truncated` false, connection intact — the case Decision #113 exists for, since Step 2.1's 64 KB cap would have truncated the largest *legal* task's result. Over-cap results truncate and still complete. **Retention is enforced, not just documented (#115):** four aged bodies, one sweep, `result_retention_purged rows: 4`, after which **9 tasks were still `COMPLETED` with timestamps intact and 4 carried `result_id` NULL** — the audit trail outlives its body through the existing `ON DELETE SET NULL`. **One correlation id now spans enqueue → assigned → acknowledged → started → progress → completed across both services** (§11); 2.4's trace stopped at `capacity`. **Phase 3 columns still untouched:** `lease_expires_at` NULL and `attempt_count` 0 on every completed row — 2.5 *reads* `attempt_count` to put `attempt` on the wire, which is not writing it. **Two defects were found by the live run that neither review nor the suite caught, and both were invisible to a passing test (#116, #117).** #116: a completing task submitted its result *and* woke the retry loop, which resent it a millisecond later — both landed, the second as a harmless `duplicate`, so nothing failed and the only cost was **87 KB duplicated per full-size task, fleet-wide**. #117: `attach` set the wake event on reconnect but the retry branch was parked on a different event, so a worker that had climbed its backoff during an outage kept sleeping after the coordinator returned — **26.23s late before the fix, 32 µs after** (`ws_connected` 05:17:56.405738, delivery 05:17:56.405770, acknowledged 131 ms later). **The exit criterion passed on the pre-fix build in both cases**, which is exactly why they are recorded: a criterion can be satisfied by something quietly wasteful or quietly slow. **One schema change — migration `0003`, a single index** (`ix_task_results_submitted_at`) for the retention sweep; Phase 2.1's table shape needed no change, as it was designed not to. **The §6 dashboard surface is DEFERRED to Step 2.7 by explicit user decision 2026-07-31 (#118)** — the dashboard shows no queue depth, no running count and no completed count, so **Step 2.5's visible behaviour cannot be watched in a browser** and was verified through the admin API and the database instead. The minimum tile row was offered and declined; recorded as a **user scope call on §6 and §7, not as a satisfied criterion** (§10). **SHIPPED AND VERIFIED OVER THE PUBLIC INTERNET 2026-07-31 (§8).** PR #36 merged, `main` at **`94636a6`**, **CI green on all 7 checks with 253 passed in CI** against ephemeral Postgres/Redis — the same count as locally. `staging / deploy` succeeded and was **checked rather than taken off CD's tick**: public `/health` returns `94636a61b994ef00f1807eee0411cdd03afe335c` **with no `-k`**, so the Let's Encrypt certificate genuinely validated. The Internet worker was **the ghcr image CI built for that SHA** (`sha256:23580cfb…`), not a local build, run against `https://dcds-staging.centralindia.cloudapp.azure.com` with `WORKER_CA_FILE` empty so the OS trust store validated the coordinator. **All four types reached `COMPLETED` over the real network**, enqueued through the public ingress and read back through the public admin API: `count_to_n` → `2000` (worker 0.003s / observed 0.113s), `hash_rounds` → `c4773d4f7ba4…` (0.378 / 0.457), `sleep(8)` → `8.0` (8.002 / 8.081), `opaque_payload` → its exact base64 round trip (0.002 / 0.096). **`hash_rounds` returned the same digest recomputed independently outside the system**, so correctness holds on the shipped artefact over the real network, not only locally; every envelope carried `session_epoch` 1 and `attempt_number` 0 and every result acked `transitioned` with `pending: 0`. `demo-worker` was scaled to 0 for the test to remove attribution ambiguity (it still runs pre-2.3 `b1963f90` and parks what it is given) and **restored to 1**. **DEPLOYED TO PRODUCTION TOO — CD run `30608814126` completed `success` on BOTH `staging / deploy` and `production / deploy`**, the gate having been approved during the session. Both production coordinator replicas are `Running` on image `…-coordinator:94636a61b99…` with `GIT_SHA=94636a61b99…`. **Honest limit on that check (§10):** production's version was **read from the Deployment spec, not from a `/health` response** — the `kubectl exec` into a production pod was denied by the harness permission classifier, while the same command against staging was allowed. Strong evidence, but not the same standard staging was held to; re-verify from inside a production pod when the cluster is next up. **Step 2.5 is DONE and APPROVED by the user 2026-07-31 (Decision #120).** The approval was given by direction — "mark 2.5 as done" — after the user stated they would run the demo themselves. **Recorded honestly (§10): §15 items 3–4 were NOT observed by the agent.** No demo or failure demo was run in the agent's presence, and none is reported here as a result. This is a **user scope call**, the same family as Decisions #34–35, #77 and the 2.2 / 2.2.1 / 2.3 approvals, and a **weaker** basis than Step 2.4's, which the user demonstrated personally. Everything else on the step is objectively verified above. The §6 dashboard surface remains deferred to Step 2.7 (#118). |
| 2.6 | Operator task APIs | **BUILT and VERIFIED LOCALLY 2026-07-31 — AWAITING APPROVAL.** All 6 exit criteria met against a real coordinator, worker, Postgres and Redis over TLS in Docker (compose project `dcds26`), driven from a container on the same network so the private dev CA validated. Design decisions **#122–#127**. Suite **275 passed** (was 253 at 2.5), `ruff` clean. **What was added:** `GET /tasks` with AND-combining filters (repeatable `status`, `task_type`, `worker_id`, `correlation_id`), `limit`/`offset` paging with `has_more` and no total (#124), `POST /tasks/{id}/cancel` for queued work only (#122), a `timeline` on `GET /tasks/{id}` reconstructed from four timestamp columns with each entry naming its source (#123), a per-source-IP rate limit applied before authentication with the dequeue primitive exempt (#125), and an app-wide handler that stops validation errors quoting the request body back (#126) — the exact path that leaked `ADMIN_SECRET` in session 13. **Migration `0004`**: `tasks.started_at` plus `ix_tasks_created_at` on `(created_at, id)`; no index for `correlation_id`/`assigned_worker_id` because 0002 already created both, found by the migration failing live on a duplicate relation. **Measured, not asserted:** a 1,000-task batch accepted in **0.069s** and paged back by correlation id as **1,000 unique ids across 5 pages** with no repeats or gaps; a `count_to_n(2000)` task executed by the real worker read back `COMPLETED` with the known answer **2000** and a four-state timeline (`10:04:30.103471/.113323/.130120/.148877`), coordinator-observed **0.036s** against worker-reported **0.002s**; with the worker stopped so nothing raced the drain, cancelling one of 3 queued tasks moved depth **377 → 376, exactly one**, with `assigned_worker_id` NULL; a second cancel **409 CANCELLED**, a COMPLETED task **409 COMPLETED**, and after restarting the worker a task it had taken **409 RUNNING** and still RUNNING afterwards; 401 on all five endpoints unauthenticated and on a one-character-short credential; the session 13 leak reproduced deliberately and the 422 came back **without the secret**; rate limit **300 × 200 then the first 429 on request #301** against a fresh window. Index cost measured both ways on 60,000 rows: the listing query **20.172 ms** with no index, **4.516 ms** on `(created_at)`, **0.096 ms** on `(created_at, id)`; bulk enqueue of 10,000 **0.540s / 0.628s / 0.593s** respectively, so ~**+0.05s** with the two index shapes inside each other's spread. Migration chain re-run **from an empty database** to `alembic_version = 0004`; `lease_expires_at` and `attempt_count` still written by nothing. **SHIPPED the same day:** PR #40 merged, `main` at **`34d8a0486e7ebaed93ad89ef5539d5eb553d88a0`**, CI green on all 7 checks with **275 passed in CI** (read from the job log, not inferred), branch deleted local and remote, and CD run `30623912130` **`success` on BOTH staging and production**. Verified on the running system rather than off CD's tick: public staging `/health` returns the merge SHA **with no `-k`**; unauthenticated `GET /tasks` and `POST /tasks/{id}/cancel` return **401 rather than 405/404**, which a pre-2.6 image cannot do; an authenticated `GET /tasks?status=COMPLETED&limit=2` over the public endpoint returned real rows with filters echoed and `has_more: true`, `/tasks/depth` returned `{depth: 0, counts: {COMPLETED: 5, RUNNING: 4, ASSIGNED: 20636}}`, and `?status=RUNING` returned **400**; `alembic_version` reads **0004** with `started_at` and `ix_tasks_created_at btree (created_at, id)` in **both** namespaces. Decision #123 confirmed against production data, unflatteringly: pre-`0004` rows come back with `started_at: null` and no RUNNING entry rather than a fabricated one. **Two carried-over limitations closed**, because `kubectl exec` into a production pod was permitted this time where sessions 14 and 15 were denied — production **reports its own version** from `/health` inside the pod rather than it being read off the Deployment spec, and `coordinator_admin_credential_separate` reads **1.0 on production**. **Not claimed (§10):** **no worker outside the local network was run for this step**, because none of its six criteria needs one — the operator API was driven over the public Internet from outside the cluster, but §8 in its literal form is not claimed here. **§6 is NOT satisfied**: this step adds no dashboard surface, so its behaviour is not watchable in a browser; that is the user's standing scope call from Decision #118, which deferred task lifecycle views to Step 2.7, and is recorded as a deferral rather than a met criterion. A latent test-isolation defect surfaced and was fixed test-side only: `assignment._work_available` is a module-level `asyncio.Event` bound to the first loop that awaits it, which a second `TestClient` module inherits dead — production has one loop for the life of the process and was not changed. **The demo and failure demo (§15 items 3–4) are OUTSTANDING**, deferred by the user 2026-07-31 to be run together with Step 2.7's, when the same behaviour is watchable in a browser — a **user scope call, not a satisfied criterion** (§10). **APPROVED by the user 2026-07-31 (Decision #128)** on that recorded evidence, with the demo still outstanding and §6 still unsatisfied by this step — recorded as a user scope call on §15 items 3–4, not as an observed demo. |
| 2.7 | Dashboard v2 — task lifecycle views | **DONE and APPROVED by the user 2026-08-03, and MERGED the same day.** Design decisions **#129–#135**, delegated to the agent by the user (recorded in #128 as a scope call on §9's approval half). All 7 exit criteria measured against a real coordinator, dashboard, worker fleet, Postgres and Redis over TLS in Docker Compose project `dcds27`; suite **288 passed** (was 275), `ruff` clean, one migration (`0005`, `ix_tasks_completed_at`). **This is the step that closes §6 for M2** — the deferral from 2.5 and 2.6 (#118) ends here. Built: a task console at **`/ui/tasks`** (#129, a second page because the ingress owns the `/tasks` prefix and a page there would work only in Compose), six dashboard proxies keeping `ADMIN_SECRET` server-side, a **cross-site forgery guard on the new write paths** (#130 — edge basic auth is attached by the browser to cross-site requests too, so it authenticates the browser, not the intent), a coordinator `GET /tasks/throughput` querying `completed_at` (#131), 2s/15s polling (#132), and server-side paging and filters (#133). **Measured, not asserted:** a task chosen *because it was still QUEUED* watched through `QUEUED -> RUNNING -> COMPLETED` while depth drained **10 → 6 → 2 → 0**; per-worker current task **16 of 16** correct against the task rows; browser submission **201** and the same request without the page header **403 without reaching the coordinator**; the four-entry timeline with the source column for each; the throughput chart matching the database bucket for bucket (7 of 8 at 2,278 completions, the miss being the still-filling minute), on an `Index Only Scan` at **0.714 ms**. **Failure demo run:** cancel a running task → 409 naming its state; cancel twice → 200 then 409; write without the header → 403; coordinator stopped under a running task → `coordinator_unreachable` and the banner, then resumption with no reload **and the in-flight task completing on reconnect**. **Two findings from the live run, both recorded honestly:** the connection pool had never been sized since Phase 1.2 and pinned at 15 while pages took up to 48.8s against a 0.198 ms query (**#134 — fixed, and the fix is reported as partial because p95 stayed at 9.9s**), and the residual is one coordinator process at **76–91% of a core** serving 95 WebSocket sessions, isolated by re-running the identical burst at 4 workers for **median 0.025s / CPU 1.84%** (**#135 — the degradation tracks fleet size, not task count; §3.9 horizontal scaling is the answer and Step 2.8's harness owns the number**). **NOT claimed:** no browser screenshot (Playwright cannot validate the private dev CA; both pages, the stylesheet and both scripts' parsing were verified instead), **§8 not claimed**, and **no CI run and no deployment** at the time this row was first written. **APPROVED by the user 2026-08-03 — by direction, on the recorded evidence: neither 2.7's nor 2.6's demo or failure demo was run in the agent's presence, and none is claimed. Recorded as a user scope call on §15 items 3–4, not as an observed demo (§10)** — the same weaker form as Decisions #120 and #128, and weaker than Step 2.4's. **MERGED 2026-08-03 as PR #42**: head `6f22c4c60c157b322882cf7041d306781102ff2b`, **all 14 CI checks SUCCESS on that exact SHA** (verified through the API, not assumed), merge commit **`2c4ce5d8eeeee08e31b5e0eea2ed41382f05192f`** on `main`, branch `phase-2.7-dashboard-v2` deleted local and remote. **DEPLOYED to BOTH environments 2026-08-03 and verified rather than taken off CD's tick (#137)** — CD run `30783222199` (`2c4ce5d`) and CD run `30784321149` (`36dbd61`, the approval-record commit) both `success` on **`staging / deploy` and `production / deploy`**, and public staging `/health` returns **`36dbd61ec170f080b1bf4c3aa97cf9c234b935e4`** with **no `-k`**, so the Let's Encrypt certificate genuinely validated. **Production rests on CD's tick alone — not independently checked this session.** **Still not claimed: §8** (no worker outside the local network for 2.7), **and the user-run demo and failure demo for 2.6 and 2.7.** |
| 2.8 | Load testing harness | **DONE and APPROVED by the user 2026-08-03 (Decision #147), on recorded evidence — its demo and failure demo were NOT run in the agent's presence and remain outstanding, alongside 2.6's and 2.7's.** Built, verified, merged and deployed the same day. **PR #43 merged as `7dce17fc85ecd580783c1f5645a3b9551ca00941`**, all 14 CI checks green on head `c0169fa`, branch deleted local and remote. **CI `success` and CD `success` on BOTH `staging / deploy` and `production / deploy`** for the merge commit (runs `30789275025` and `30789329354`). **Verified rather than taken off CD's tick: public staging `/health` returns `7dce17fc85ecd580783c1f5645a3b9551ca00941` with no `-k`**, so the Let's Encrypt certificate validated and the coordinator reported its own version. **Production was NOT independently checked** and rests on CD's tick alone — the same weaker form as Steps 2.5, 2.6 and 2.7. **The scheduled load-test workflow is no longer merely configured: it is registered (`Load test`, id 325979287) and has now RUN GREEN end to end on a hosted runner** (`workflow_dispatch` run `30790101364`, every step `success`, teardown clean) — 20 workers, **2,000 / 2,000 `COMPLETED`, 2,000 stored results, 2,000 distinct rows, 0 duplicate assignments, PASS**, at 129.2 tasks/second. That closes the one gap left open at the time of the build: before the merge GitHub had not registered the workflow at all, because `schedule` and `workflow_dispatch` only take effect from the default branch. Design decisions **#138–#146**, all six exit criteria measured, **no application code changed**. Ships `scripts/loadtest.py` (four scenarios — burst, sustained, mixed, saturation — one JSON report, its own pass/fail verdict), `tests/test_loadtest.py` (**31 tests**, suite **319 passed**, was 288), `.github/workflows/loadtest.yml` (weekly plus `workflow_dispatch`, deliberately **not** a required check on `main`, #143), and `docs/load-testing.md`. **The headline criterion, counted from the coordinator's own rows rather than the harness's tally (#140): 10,000 tasks across 100 workers, three times, 10,000 / 10,000 `COMPLETED` every time with 10,000 stored results and 0 duplicate assignments.** Throughput 114.0 / 84.5 / 111.3 tasks/s. **Reproducibility is recorded in its honest form (§10): the pass/fail properties reproduce perfectly, the throughput figure reproduces to ±26%** — and what *is* stable is the coordinator at **95.6–97.6% of one core in every run**. **The saturation point is `~110–124 tasks/s for one coordinator process, reached at FIVE workers`**, measured across a 5/10/25/50/100 ramp where throughput was **flat and lower at 100 than at 10** — which is a **different** answer from Decision #135's and is recorded as such (#141); both are the same single-process ceiling seen from two directions, and §3.9 horizontal scaling remains the answer. Component attribution measured rather than inferred: **coordinator 92–112% of a core, Postgres 43–60%, Redis 3–7%, the harness itself 45.3%** — so neither the host nor the harness is the bound. The honest per-task latency comes from `sustained` at a rate the pipeline serves: **60/s held exactly, p50 0.39s / p95 0.71s / p99 1.43s**; at 150/s the queue climbs to 2,116 and **still loses nothing**. **§8 satisfied: 300 / 300 `COMPLETED` over the public ingress with no `-k` and no `--insecure`**, p50 1.745s / p95 3.128s, 0 duplicates — deliberately a small fleet, because staging's shipped `REGISTER_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE` of 5 per source IP was **left as deployed** rather than weakened for a test (#142). **Three defects were found by live runs and none by review or the suite** — `queue_kept_up` judged on the wrong window and passed a saturated pipeline (#144), an unbounded shutdown that hung nineteen minutes after the work was done (#145), and a drain check that assumed the harness owned the whole fleet and could never finish against a real deployment (#146). **NOT claimed:** no fault injection, no multi-host fleet, no asserted performance budget, and every published figure is from one laptop (Intel i5-4460S, 4 cores). |
| 2.9 | M2 demo and verification | **DONE 2026-08-03. ALL NINE EXIT CRITERIA SATISFIED — but two of them in a form weaker than their own words, stated plainly (Decision #150, superseding the partial close in #148).** After the initial close, the user directed that the skipped failure demos be performed rather than carried, and **all EIGHTEEN documented failure-demo items across Steps 2.6, 2.7, 2.8 and 2.9 were run against a real stack** (compose project `dcds29` — coordinator, dashboard, Postgres, Redis and a real worker over TLS). Suite **321 passed** (was 320 in CI at 2.8), `ruff` clean. **The two weaker forms, named not smoothed:** (a) **the demos were run by the AGENT, not by the user** — §15 item 4 asks for the user's hands, and the user's own 2026-08-03 demo covered the success path and was not observed; (b) **no remote Internet worker took part in any of it** — everything was local Docker, so the criterion's "including remote Internet workers" clause is **NOT claimed**; §8's own satisfaction stands from Step 2.8 (300/300 over the public ingress with a validated certificate) and was not re-demonstrated. **Criteria newly MEASURED this session rather than cited:** **correlation-ID traceability** — one id (`3d58d7cb…`) carries five coordinator events (`tasks_enqueued` → `task_assigned` → `task_acknowledged` → `task_started` → `task_completed`) and three worker events, with `GET /tasks?correlation_id=` returning the batch by that id alone; **zero duplicate assignments at the criterion's OWN number** — `burst --workers 5 --tasks 5000` gave **5,000/5,000 `COMPLETED`, 5,000 stored results, 5,000 distinct rows, 0 duplicate assignments**, where before it was met only by the harder 10,000-task run; **zero task loss counted in every scenario run** — 5,000/5,000, 18,000/18,000 at an offered 300/s, 2,000/2,000 across a coordinator restart, and 10,000 accounted for **exactly** when the coordinator was killed mid-drain (1,689 `COMPLETED` + 8,265 `QUEUED` + 20 `ASSIGNED` + 26 `RUNNING`); and **the §13 fresh-clone run, previously never performed for M2** — a clone carrying no `.env` and no `certs/` brought up through **only the three documented README steps** to 5/5 containers, `/health` healthy, `/ready` db+redis ok, dashboard **200**, a worker `ONLINE`, and a `count_to_n(2000)` task `QUEUED` → `COMPLETED` in **~198 ms**, with **zero undocumented manual steps**. **The headline failure-demo measurements:** the 10-minute criterion passed in one window per Decision #100 — `sleep(600)` `COMPLETED` at a coordinator-observed **600.037s** (worker-reported 600.001s) with the worker **`ONLINE` in all 118 samples**, **114 heartbeats and a worst gap of 10.52s** against the 12s SUSPECT threshold, **a margin of only 1.48s, far tighter than Step 2.4's 5.26s because that worker was pinned to `--cpus=1` while this one saturated four slots on a 4-core laptop — it passed and it is not comfortable**; over-capacity load at an offered 300/s drove `queue_kept_up` **false** with depth climbing **0 → 12,840** (max 12,956) and **still lost nothing**; the registration limiter refused **55 of 100** workers with 429 and **505** `registration_rate_limited` events; seven classes of malformed submission were all refused with **not one becoming a row**; an unsupported task type stayed **`QUEUED` and visible** while a supported one submitted in the same breath completed with the correct answer, proving the filter **selective, not inert**; and a coordinator restart left queue depth **byte-identical** at 2,000 before and after, still drainable to 0. **ONE REAL DEFECT WAS FOUND, and by running the demo rather than by review (#149):** Step 2.8's own documented "stop the coordinator mid-drain" demo made the harness die with a **bare `URLError` traceback — zero bytes on stdout, no JSON report, no verdict at all**, which is the one outcome a harness whose entire job is honest reporting must never produce. Fixed in `main()` so every scenario is covered; after the fix it exits **1** with `FAIL: coordinator_reachable_throughout` and writes its report. **Two non-defects worth keeping:** the task-API limiter's fixed 60-second window lives **in Redis and survives a coordinator restart**, so a rate-limit demo re-run too soon sees the previous window's count; and `burst --workers 0` **wrongly PASSED** on first run because the stack's own worker container drained the tasks — the documented demo assumes an empty fleet, Decision #146's lesson from the other side. **STILL NOT DONE:** no fault injection, no multi-host fleet, no asserted performance budget, every figure from one laptop (Intel i5-4460S, 4 cores), and **CI had not yet run on this branch's own commits** at the time of writing. **Status at session close 2026-08-03: the three commits are PUSHED to `origin/docs/m2-close` but NO PR is open and `main` is still at `39d8360`, so CI has still never run on them and the "CI green" criterion is the one item of 2.9 whose evidence is outstanding.** Two consequences worth knowing before the merge: `PHASE_STATE.md` on `main` still reads 2.9 NOT STARTED and M2 IN PROGRESS, and the scheduled `Load test` workflow runs from the **default branch**, so it keeps using the pre-#149 harness — the one that dies with a bare traceback — until this lands. **--- The earlier partial close, superseded but kept per the append-only spirit, follows. ---** **CLOSED on recorded evidence 2026-08-03 by explicit user direction (Decision #148) — NOT a full verification run, and two exit criteria are UNMET.** The user ran the demo personally on 2026-08-03 and reported it worked; **it was not observed by the agent and the remote-Internet-worker half was not stated, so neither is claimed** (§10). **What is objectively met.** (3) **Correlation-ID traceability** — met at Step 2.5, where one id spans enqueue → assigned → acknowledged → started → progress → completed across coordinator and worker, and at Step 2.2, where one id spanned two different AKS replicas; **not re-run for 2.9**. (4) **Zero duplicate assignments** — met and *exceeded* at Step 2.8: 10,000 tasks across 100 workers, three runs, **0 duplicate assignments every time**, counted from the coordinator's own rows rather than the harness's tally (#140); **a run at exactly 5,000 was never performed**, so the criterion is satisfied by a strictly harder measurement and not by its own number. (5) **Zero task loss** — 10,000/10,000 `COMPLETED` three times, 2,000/2,000 on a hosted CI runner, 300/300 over the public ingress, and 0 lost at an offered 150/s with the queue at 2,116; **not proven for scenarios never run** (no fault injection, no multi-host fleet). (6) **CI green including load test** — CI **`success`** on `main` head **`39d8360ff04b21829f5e1963bc5b2f373d56973e`** (run `30791973410`) and CD **`success`** on the same SHA (run `30792021504`), both read from the API rather than assumed; the **`Load test` workflow ran `success` on a hosted runner** (run `30790101364`) with 20 workers, 2,000/2,000 `COMPLETED`, 0 duplicates at 129.2 tasks/s — **at the previous SHA `7dce17f`, and it is deliberately not a required check on `main` (#143)**. (8) **Measured throughput and latency, recorded here as the criterion requires** — all from Step 2.8 on one laptop (Intel i5-4460S, 4 cores), none re-measured for 2.9: **single-coordinator saturation ~110–124 tasks/second, reached at FIVE workers**, flat and lower at 100 workers than at 10; the three 10,000-task runs at **114.0 / 84.5 / 111.3 tasks/s**, i.e. **reproducible only to ±26%**, while the coordinator sat at **95.6–97.6% of one core in every run** (the stable figure); component attribution **coordinator 92–112% of a core, Postgres 43–60%, Redis 3–7%, the harness itself 45.3%**; honest per-task latency from `sustained` at a rate the pipeline serves — **60/s held exactly at p50 0.39s / p95 0.71s / p99 1.43s**, against a burst p50 of **45s** that is almost entirely queue wait; and over the public ingress **p50 1.745s / p95 3.128s** at 300/300. (9) **Approval** — given 2026-08-03 (#148). **What is UNMET, named rather than waived quietly.** (1) **The demo is PARTIAL** — user-run and unobserved, remote Internet workers not claimed; §8 itself was separately satisfied at Step 2.8. (2) **The full failure demo was NOT performed** — carried unsatisfied since Step 2.6, now across five sessions, alongside 2.6's, 2.7's and 2.8's. (7) **The §13 fresh-clone run was NOT performed at milestone level** — Step 2.8 verified a fresh clone for **the load harness only** (clone → venv → the documented command → 400/400, PASS), which is not the whole platform. **Nothing new was built, measured or run for 2.9; this row is a closing record, not a result.** |

### Milestone 3 — Fault Tolerance
| Phase | Title | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 3.0 | Design gate — failure taxonomy and guarantees | **DONE and APPROVED by the user 2026-08-03** — designed the same day, Decisions #152–#168, full record in `docs/phase-3-fault-tolerance.md` §3.0. All 14 required failure entries have a coordinator-observed detection mechanism, a recovery action and a dashboard outcome; five failures are explicitly NOT recovered; the guarantee is at-least-once execution with exactly-once completion. **Approved before any code was written, which is what §9's "short architecture gate, then build" asks for.** **All three §16 escalations were approved with it (#169–#171), on the user's instruction to take whichever variant costs the least design and implementation time and adds no load to the running system:** no `session_epoch` fencing (#169), no Redis dedup store (#170), and the Postgres backup gap accepted as an owned, dated risk that M3 does not address (#171). **Two of the three REMOVE work the step wording would otherwise have required, and none of the three adds a query, a store, a background job, a migration or a protocol message.** The Step 3.3 and Step 3.4 wording each deviation contradicted was corrected in the same commit, so no step now asks for something the gate decided against. **Steps 3.1 and 3.2 are built on this gate and follow it; Steps 3.3–3.9 remain NOT STARTED and are not to begin without an explicit go-ahead (§9).** |
| 3.1 | Lease and timeout engine | **DONE, APPROVED by the user 2026-08-04, MERGED (PR #51, merge commit `139185448eb8a7593060afd1dbb51f1481674918`) and DEPLOYED to BOTH environments** — CD run `30883913007` succeeded on `staging / deploy` and `production / deploy`, public staging `/health` returned that SHA with no `-k`, and production's in-cluster assert (Decision #151) reported the same. **Built and verified 2026-08-04.** Decisions #172-#177, full record in `docs/phase-3-fault-tolerance.md` §3.1.1-§3.1.6. **All six exit criteria measured, not asserted:** the two published detection bounds were tested against their own numbers - a killed worker's task reclaimed in **31.2s** against a <=35s bound, and a *frozen* worker's (no close frame observed at all) in **49.6s** against a <=65s bound; a `sleep(600)` task held its lease across four-plus lease windows and was never reclaimed; **three real coordinator replicas drained 3,000 expired leases in 5.74s (~522/s) with exactly 3,000 reclaim log lines, 0 tasks reclaimed twice and 0 wrong attempt counts**; and a per-type execution cap was changed from 300s to 1800s through `PUT /tasks/policies/{type}` with nothing restarted, the next claim carrying a 1796s cap. Suite **368 passed** (was 323), `ruff` clean. Migration **0006** carries the whole milestone's schema so Steps 3.2-3.4 need none, and its backfill finally closes Decision #91's ~20,636 stranded rows as `FAILED` rather than flooding the queue with them. **A live run found a defect review and the suite both missed - the sixth time (#172)** - and two more were found reviewing this step's own implementation (#174, and the `recovered_tasks` fix); each has a regression test that fails against the first version. **Deliberately NOT done and named rather than discovered: no retry policy** (no attempt cap, no backoff, no worker exclusion - so a task whose worker is permanently gone cycles instead of reaching `FAILED`), no `task_cancel`, no fencing, no recovery timeline in the GUI, **no remote Internet worker (so §8 is not claimed for this step)**, and **every demo was agent-run, not user-run** (§15 items 3-4). |
| 3.2 | Reassignment and retry | **DONE and APPROVED by the user 2026-08-04 (Decision #183), on the recorded evidence — built and verified the same day.** Decisions #178-#182, full record in `docs/phase-3-fault-tolerance.md` §3.2.1-§3.2.6. **Five of six exit criteria measured, not asserted; the sixth is named as UNMET.** Against a real three-worker Compose stack on shortened windows: a killed worker's task was requeued in **5.1s** with the dead worker excluded, was **RUNNING on a different worker 2.5s later** and completed there; a poison task (`sleep(600)` against a `max_execution` lowered to 10s through the policy API with nothing restarted) burned exactly three attempts and reached **terminal `FAILED` at 37.0s** with `not_before` and `excluded_worker_id` cleared and three attempt rows explaining it; a **one-worker fleet was refused its own task for the full 30s exclusion window and then given it back at 30.7s**, which is the anti-starvation decision proven live; and `GET /workers/failures` matched the attempt rows exactly. `coordinator_tasks_reassigned_total 3` + `coordinator_tasks_exhausted_total 1` = `coordinator_leases_expired_total 4`, so no reclaim went unaccounted for. The worker honoured the best-effort `task_cancel` **0.28s** after receiving it. Suite **387 passed** (was 368), `ruff` clean, **no migration** — 0006 already carried the columns (gate §3.0.7). **Three defects were found reviewing this step before it ran**, each with a regression test: a retry that became eligible with nothing to wake the engine (a 30s stall in a recovery that was working), a completed task keeping the failed attempt's retry state, and two of this step's own assertions that would have flaked on the jitter. **Deliberately NOT done and named rather than discovered: a worker-reported `task_failed` is still terminal and is not retried** (so a *transient* executor failure gets no second chance), no fencing (3.4), no worker quarantine (Phase 4), no fleet-wide failure view (3.7), **no remote Internet worker — so §8 is NOT claimed and that exit criterion is recorded UNMET** — and **every demo was agent-run, not user-run** (§15 items 3-4). |
| 3.3 | Idempotency and duplicate suppression | **DONE and APPROVED by the user 2026-08-05 (Decision #187), on the recorded evidence rather than on a user-run demo — built and verified 2026-08-04.** Decisions #184-#186, full record in `docs/phase-3-fault-tolerance.md` §3.3.1-§3.3.6. **All six exit criteria measured, not asserted.** **No migration, no new table, no new Redis key, no new metric and no protocol change** — the step is a reordering of two guards in `complete_task`, one more column on a row it was already locking, and a token comparison on the rare terminal branch. What it changes is that the coordinator now tells the truth about *which* submission it kept: a retry of the stored submission is `duplicate` (`accepted: true`), and a result it will never store — because another attempt won, or because the task was already `FAILED` — is **`superseded`** (`accepted: false`), where 2.5 answered those `duplicate`, `not_owner` and `illegal` respectively. **The two misleading answers only became reachable when Step 3.2 made reassignment real**, and `not_owner` in particular is the answer §12 gives an impostor. Measured against a **three-replica** Compose stack: twenty identical submissions of one envelope gave one `transitioned` and 19 `duplicate` with **one result row and an unmoved `completed_at`**; a different envelope for the same completed task was refused `superseded` with the winner's token and body still stored; **the same envelope re-sent to a different replica answered `duplicate`**, proving suppression across processes that share only Postgres. **The race was reproduced deliberately rather than described**: a `sleep(40)` task's worker was frozen with `docker pause`, the task was reclaimed and requeued at **12.2s**, completed by a different worker by **55.8s**, and the original — unpaused, having genuinely finished its own 40.001s of work — was answered `superseded` **40.9s after that completion**, logging `task_result_refused / pending: 0` and writing nothing. **The task executed twice and completed once, which is the gate's delivery guarantee (§3.0.5) demonstrated end to end.** **The ledger held under load and under a deliberate race storm:** across two bursts and a run in which the type's lease was set below its execution time so that **every attempt of 60 tasks lost its task**, the database ended at **1,303 `COMPLETED` = 1,303 result rows = 1,303 distinct idempotency tokens, with 0 result rows shared by two tasks and 0 `FAILED` tasks carrying a result**. The storm's accounting is exact: **181 lease expiries = 121 reassignments + 60 exhaustions**, and the 180 late results those lost attempts submitted were answered **120 `not_owner`** (task still live) **+ 60 `superseded`** (task already terminal) = 180 = 60 x 3 attempts, **none of which added a row**. **"No dedup store" is asserted rather than assumed**: the schema is exactly the migrations' six tables, a test enumerates them so adding one fails CI, and a 20-duplicate storm created **not one Redis key** (12 -> 18 keys across the run, every one attributable to the probe registering as a new worker plus two rate-limit counters). Suite **400 passed** (was 387 at 3.2), `ruff` clean. **Two things running it found:** the exclusion window expiring makes the loser a legal destination for its own task, so the first race test got it back — correct behaviour, wrong test, fixed by dropping the loser's socket; and a **cold start of three replicas against an empty database races the first migration** (`duplicate key value violates unique constraint "pg_type_typname_nsp_index"`), which predates M3, cannot happen on an already-migrated database, and now has a documented start order. **Deliberately NOT done and named rather than discovered: no fencing** — a stale result from an *earlier attempt* of a task the same worker holds again is still accepted, and a late result for a task that is still live is still `not_owner` with no attempt row (both are Step 3.4's criteria); **no dashboard change**, because the task drawer already renders the winning submission's `idempotency_token` beside the attempt row that records who lost, and a per-task duplicate counter would need exactly the store this step's fourth criterion forbids (fleet-wide totals are on `/metrics` and belong to Step 3.7); **a duplicate arriving after retention has purged the body reads as `superseded`**, because the token goes with the body; **no remote Internet worker — §8 is NOT claimed**; and **every demo was agent-run, not user-run** (§15 items 3-4). |
| 3.4 | Stale result fencing | **DONE and APPROVED by the user 2026-08-05 (Decision #193), on the recorded evidence rather than on a user-run demo — built and verified the same day.** Committed as `bf1e819` on branch `phase-3.4-fencing` and opened as **PR #54 against `main`, 14 of 14 checks pass, `MERGEABLE` / `CLEAN` — NOT MERGED, so no environment runs it.** Decisions #188-#192, full record in `docs/phase-3-fault-tolerance.md` §3.4.1-§3.4.6. **All six exit criteria measured, not asserted.** **No migration, no new table, no new Redis key and NO PROTOCOL CHANGE — checkable rather than claimed: `git status` for this step lists eight files and not one is under `worker/`.** The rule is one comparison inside the `FOR UPDATE` lock `complete_task` already held, on two columns its row read already fetched: **a result is accepted if and only if the sender is the current `assigned_worker_id` AND its `attempt_number` equals `tasks.attempt_count`**. This closes the hole Step 3.3 named and left open — a stale result from an *earlier attempt* of a task the same worker holds again was **accepted** before this step, because ownership cannot catch a submission from the worker the row names — and corrects a shipped answer: a late result for a task still live but reassigned away was `not_owner`, the answer §12 reserves for an impostor, with no durable record that real work had been discarded. Each fence writes **one `task_attempts` row** (`FENCED` / `stale_attempt` or `task_reassigned`), which is what makes it inspectable; the gate's named metric `coordinator_results_fenced_total{reason}` and its "any sustained fencing" alert both shipped (§3.0.9). **Measured against a real Compose stack over TLS: `task_reassigned` reproduced by cutting the holding worker off the Docker network so the reclaim's `task_cancel` had nowhere to go** — the task was reclaimed three times and completed by a second worker, and the original, reconnected, was refused with `task_result_fenced attempt_number=0 session_epoch=2` while the shipped worker logged `task_result_refused / was_pending: true / pending: 0` and did not crash; the task still reached **`COMPLETED`**, so it **executed four times and completed once**, which is the gate's delivery guarantee end to end. **`stale_attempt` reproduced twice** (08:38:19 and 08:40:29 UTC), both `attempt_number=0` with the fenced worker being the same worker the row names as current assignee. **The amended criterion (#169) measured on the accepting side: a result executed under session epoch 7 and submitted on epoch 8, after a coordinator restart mid-execution, reached `COMPLETED` with `attempt_count: 0` and no abnormal endings** — the phase plan's original wording would have made that measured, correct behaviour a rejection. Visibility is on both reads the task console performs: a **fenced results** tile on `/ui/tasks` driven by a new `fenced_results` field on `GET /tasks/depth`, counted from `task_attempts` rather than from the Prometheus counter so every replica answers identically and the figure survives a restart — **and the counter's reset was measured: after a coordinator restart `coordinator_results_fenced_total` has no series at all.** Suite **414 passed** (was 400 at 3.3), `ruff` clean, `helm lint` clean. **Running it found three things:** the first fence insert was refused by asyncpg with `AmbiguousParameterError: inconsistent types deduced for parameter $4 (text versus character varying)`, which nothing short of executing the statement would have caught; one of this step's own assertions was too strong and asserted the **opposite** of the step (that no Redis key changed across a fence storm, when the correct behaviour is for `worker:{id}:current_tasks` to go away as the credit is released); and, most usefully, **Step 3.2's `task_cancel` makes the stale-attempt fence hard to reach on a healthy socket, because the worker cancels the superseded execution before it can produce a stale result at all** — so fencing is a backstop for the case the cancel cannot arrive, which is by definition the case the task was reclaimed for. **Recorded honestly (§10): the live reproduction of `stale_attempt` is timing-dependent and did not fire on every attempt** — four later runs of the same recipe produced `task_reassigned` or no fence; the branch is deterministic in `tests/test_fencing.py` against a real Postgres. **Deliberately NOT done and named rather than discovered: `task_failed` is not fenced** (Decision #192 — it does not carry `attempt_number`, so fencing it would break this step's own no-protocol-change criterion), `task_started` is not fenced (it can only re-stamp a timestamp), **no penalty, counter or quarantine for a worker that lies about its attempt number** (§12's quarantine is Phase 4), no per-reason breakdown on the dashboard tile, nothing on the fleet view (3.7), **no remote Internet worker — so §8 is NOT claimed for this step** — and **every demo was agent-run, not user-run** (§15 items 3-4). |
| 3.5 | Coordinator restart and recovery | **DONE and APPROVED by the user 2026-08-05 (Decision #199), on the recorded evidence — built and verified the same day — and FIVE of six exit criteria are met, with the sixth named UNMET rather than waived.** Decisions #194-#198, full record in `docs/phase-3-fault-tolerance.md` §3.5.1-§3.5.6. Opened as **PR #55, stacked on PR #54 rather than on `main`** (`phase-3.5-restart-recovery` is branched off `phase-3.4-fencing`); **14 of 14 checks pass, `MERGEABLE` / `CLEAN` — NOT MERGED and NOT DEPLOYED.** Suite **426 passed** (was 414 at 3.4) locally **and in CI**, `ruff` clean, `helm lint` clean. **No migration, no new table, no new Redis key, no protocol change — zero files under `worker/` were touched.** **The design gate's prediction held**: §3.0.13 committed this step to needing *no startup scan*, because recovery is 3.1's continuous reclaimer plus lease renewal on `hello`, and re-reading it against the running system confirmed that the step's first bullet is already true continuously rather than at boot. Building a boot-time scan would have added the second recovery path §3.0.2 rejected. **What did not exist was graceful shutdown, and that is the whole build**: a one-way drain flag set from the signal handler, an `assign_once` that returns 0 before it counts a pass or touches the database, a bounded wait for this replica's *unacknowledged deliveries*, and `/ready` answering **503 `draining`** ahead of its dependency checks. The container entrypoint moved from `uvicorn app.main:app` to `python -m app.serve`, a 137-line `uvicorn.Server` subclass that intercepts SIGTERM, drains, then hands off to uvicorn's own exit — one code path for Compose and Kubernetes alike, because a `preStop` hook would put half the behaviour in one environment only (§3.5). `terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 45` in the chart and `stop_grace_period: 45s` in Compose are the matching kill deadlines; a test fails if the default 15s window ever grows past them. **Measured against a real Compose stack (`dcds35`) at stock configuration, not described:** a `sleep(45)` task `RUNNING` at SIGTERM cost **176ms** of shutdown end to end (`waited_seconds: 0.0`) and then **completed on its original worker at `attempt_count: 0` with an empty attempts list** after the coordinator came back — the worker reconnected on its existing identity, epoch 1 to 2, and the coordinator renewed the lease from the database on `hello`. With a `docker pause`d worker holding an unacknowledged delivery, the drain instead held the **full 15.053s**, `timed_out: true`, `/ready` returned `503 draining` on every one-second poll from t+2s to t+12s, and the container still exited **0** rather than 137. **The 100-worker / 1,000-task restart run passed all eight of its own checks**: 1,000 rows / 1,000 distinct / 1,000 `COMPLETED` / 1,000 results with **0 redeliveries**, **100 registrations across 200 sessions** (every worker back, none re-enrolled), 380 tasks in flight at the restart, fleet reconnect p50 **17.50s** inside a 2.7s band from 276 attempts, coordinator at **28.8% of one core**, 0 rate-limited retries. The reconnect floor is 15.8s and not 1s because each failed attempt doubles the shipped worker's own backoff — a property of `WS_BACKOFF_*`, not of this step, and the price of not having a herd. **The failure demo is the same image with `SHUTDOWN_DRAIN_SECONDS=0`**: zero `shutdown_drain_started` events, `/ready` answering **200** on the last poll before the process vanished, and the delivery still unacknowledged when the socket closed. **The rolling Kubernetes upgrade was NOT MET and not waived — and is now MET, closed 2026-08-06; see §3.5.7.** It was carried because staging could not test a rolling upgrade of the drain until an image containing the drain was deployed there. When PRs #55 and #54 were merged (`main` at `31e3cba`) that deploy **failed**, and the cause was a real defect that only Kubernetes could expose: kubelet injects `<SERVICE>_PORT=tcp://<ip>:<port>` for every Service in the namespace, and Step 3.5's own entrypoint change (`uvicorn app.main:app` to `python -m app.serve`) is the first thing in the project to read `COORDINATOR_PORT` from the environment, so the coordinator parsed `tcp://10.0.67.120:8443` as an int and crashlooped, stalling the rollout at 1/3. Compose injects no service links, so all 438 tests and every local demo passed — **the unverified rollout criterion is exactly what would have caught it, which is the argument for the criterion.** A second defect was found beside it: `helm rollback` with no revision means "the one before latest", so the CD failure step rolled **forward** onto the failed revision (staging rev 61 "Rollback to 59" became rev 62 "Rollback to 60", the release reading `deployed` while pointing at a crashlooping image). Both fixed in **PR #57**, `main` at **`3c55314`** — `configmap.yaml` pins `COORDINATOR_HOST`/`COORDINATOR_PORT` the same way `POSTGRES_PORT` and `REDIS_PORT` have always been pinned (confirmed in-pod: pinned values win over the injection), the CD rollback is gated on `failure() && steps.helm.outcome == 'success'`, and new `tests/test_chart_env.py` fails if any env var the coordinator reads is left for kubelet to inject (mutation-checked). **The criterion was then measured against public staging over the real Internet with a validated certificate:** `kubectl -n staging rollout restart deploy/coordinator` replaced all three replicas while **20 tasks were in flight and 0 had completed**, returning 0 in **37.535s**, and **all eight harness checks passed — 400 enqueued / 400 read back / 400 `COMPLETED` / 400 distinct rows / 400 results, 5 workers back across 12 sessions on 5 registrations, 0 errors after first connect, queue depth max 354 to final 0, 0 rate-limited retries.** Reported honestly rather than tidied: **one redelivery out of 217 deliveries** (a rolling upgrade drains three replicas in sequence, so a delivery crossing a rollover can be reclaimed and re-sent) which produced **no duplicate row and no second result** — Steps 3.3 and 3.4 doing their job; and the fleet was **5, not 100**, because staging runs the shipped `REGISTER_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE` default of 5 per source IP and it was **left as deployed rather than weakened for the test**, the same call Decision #142 made for Step 2.8, so this proves the path and the property, not a ceiling. **Still not claimed:** the demo was agent-run rather than user-run (§15 items 3-4), and production was not rolling-upgraded under load. |
| 3.6 | Partial completion policy | **BUILT and VERIFIED 2026-08-05, AWAITING APPROVAL — all FIVE exit criteria met.** Decisions #200–#203, full record in `docs/phase-3-fault-tolerance.md` §3.6.1–§3.6.8. Policy: **discard the interrupted attempt and re-execute in full**; checkpointing deferred with the three things it would need named (§3.6.3). **No production code changed** — `tests/test_reexecution.py` (12 tests) and documentation are the whole step, suite **438 passed**. Verified live on `dcds36`: **seven executions of one 10,000,000-round `hash_rounds` workload across two workers produced ONE digest**, matching a value computed outside the repository; the reassigned task's abandoned attempt burned **28.799s** of CPU and was refused `superseded`. Purity enforced at runtime (`open`/`socket` denied) and statically (import allowlist), both confirmed to fail against a deliberately impure executor. §8 NOT claimed — no remote Internet worker; demos agent-run, not user-run. |
| 3.7 | Dashboard v3 — failure and recovery views | NOT STARTED |
| 3.8 | Chaos testing harness | NOT STARTED |
| 3.9 | M3 demo and verification | NOT STARTED |
